| PROVOST | I do it not in evil disposition,<br>But from Lord [[Angelo]] by special charge. |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | Thus can the demigod Authority<br>Make us pay down for our offence, by weight,<br>The bonds of heaven. On whom it will, it will;<br>On whom it will not, so; yet still ’tis just. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Lucio]] |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Why, how now, [[Claudio]]? Whence comes this restraint?<br><br>B. 3.1.515-4.1.65<br>Before revision there would have been no act-break and no [[Song\|song]]; the lines immediately following the [[Song\|song]] would also have been absent. The Duke’s soliloquies ‘He who the [[Sword\|sword]] of heaven will bear’ and ‘O place and [[Greatness\|greatness]]’ have evidently been transposed in revision; in the original, the end of ‘O place and [[Greatness\|greatness]]’ would have led straight on to the Duke’s meeting with [[Isabella]] and then [[Mariana]]. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | I am going to visit the prisoner. Fare you [[Well\|well]]. |
| DUKE | Peace be with you. Exit [[Escalus]]<br>O place and [[Greatness\|greatness]], millions of false eyes<br>Are stuck upon thee; volumes of report<br>Run with their false and most contrarious [[Quest\|quest]]<br>Upon thy doings; thousand escapes of wit<br>Make thee the father of their idle dream,<br>And rack thee in their fancies. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Isabella]] |
| DUKE | Very [[Well\|well]] met.<br>What is the news from this good deputy? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | He hath a [[Garden\|garden]] circummured with brick,<br>Whose western side is with a vineyard backed;<br>And to that vineyard is a planckèd gate,<br>That makes his opening with this bigger key.<br>This other doth command a little door<br>Which from the vineyard to the [[Garden\|garden]] leads.<br>There have I made my promise<br>Upon the heavy middle of the [[Night\|night]]<br>To call upon him. |
| DUKE | But shall you on your knowledge find this way? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | I have ta‘en a due and wary note upon’t.<br>With whispering and most guilty diligence,<br>In action all of precept, he did show me<br>The way twice o’er. |
| DUKE | Are there no other tokens<br>Between you ’[[Greed\|greed]] concerning her observance? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | No, none, but only a repair i’th’ dark,<br>And that I have possessed him my most stay<br>Can be but brief, for I have made him know<br>I have a servant comes with me along<br>That stays upon me, whose [[Persuasion\|persuasion]] is<br>I come about my brother. |
| DUKE | ’Tis [[Well\|well]] borne up.<br>I have not yet made known to [[Mariana]]<br>A word of this.—What ho, within! Come forth! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Mariana]] |
| DUKE | (To [[Mariana]]) I pray you be acquainted with this maid.<br>She comes to do you good. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | I do [[Desire\|desire]] the like. |
| DUKE | (to [[Mariana]])<br>Do you persuade yourself that I respect you? |
| [[Mariana\|MARIANA]] | Good friar, I know you do, and so have found it. |
| DUKE | Take then this your companion by the hand,<br>Who hath a story ready for your ear.<br>I shall attend your leisure; but make haste,<br>The vaporous [[Night\|night]] approaches. |
| [[Mariana\|MARIANA]] | Will’t please you walk aside. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Mariana]] and [[Isabella]] |
| DUKE | He who the [[Sword\|sword]] of heaven will bear<br>Should be as holy as severe,<br>Pattern in himself to know,<br>Grace to stand, and virtue go,<br>More nor less to others paying<br>Than by [[Self\|self]]-offences weighing.<br>Shame to him whose cruel striking<br>Kills for faults of his own liking!<br>Twice treble shame on [[Angelo]],<br>To weed my vice, and let his grow!<br>O, what may man within him hide,<br>Though angel on the outward side!<br>How may likeness made in crimes<br>Make my practice on the times<br>To draw with idle spiders’ strings<br>Most ponderous and substantial things?<br>Craft against vice I must apply.<br>With [[Angelo]] tonight shall lie<br>His old betrothed but despised.<br>So disguise shall, by th’ disguised,<br>Pay with falsehood false exacting,<br>And perform an old contracting. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter |
| DUKE | [[Mariana]] |
| *(didascalia)* | and [[Isabella]] |
| DUKE | Welcome. How agreed? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | She’ll take the enterprise upon her, father,<br>If you advise it. |
| [[Othello\|OTHELLO]] | [[Othello]] was given before James I in the Banqueting [[house\|House]] at Whitehall on I November 1604. Information about the Turkish invasion of Cyprus appears to derive from [[Richard]] Knolles’s [[history\|History]] of the Turks, published no earlier than 30 September 1603, so Shakespeare probably completed his play some [[Time\|time]] between that date and the [[Summer\|summer]] of 1604. It first appeared in print in a quarto of 1622; the version printed in the 1623 Folio is about 160 lines longer, and has over a thousand differences in wording. It seems that Shakespeare partially revised his play, adding, for example, [[Desdemona]]’s willow [[Song\|song]] (4.3) and building up [[Emilia]]’s role in the closing scenes. We base our text on the Folio as that seems to represent Shakespeare’s second thoughts.<br>Shakespeare’s decision to make a black man a [[Tragic Hero\|tragic hero]] was bold and original: by an ancient tradition, blackness was associated with sin and [[Death\|death]]; and blackamoors in plays before Shakespeare are generally villainous (as is [[Aaron]] in [[Titus Andronicus]]). The story of a Moorish commander deluded by his ensign (standard-bearer) into believing that his young wife has been unfaithful to him with another soldier derives from a prose tale by the Italian Giambattista Cinzio Giraldi first published in 1565 in a collection of linked tales, Gli Ecatommiti (The Hundred Tales). Shakespeare must have read it either in Italian or in a French [[Translation\|translation]] of 1584, he may have looked at both. Giraldi tells the tale in a few pages of compressed, matter-of-fact narrative interspersed with brief conversations. His main characters are a Moor of [[Venice]] ([[Othello]]), his Venetian wife ([[Desdemona]]), his ensign ([[Iago]]), his ensign’s wife ([[Emilia]]), and a corporal ([[Cassio]]) ‘who was very dear to the Moor’. Only [[Desdemona]] is named. Shakespeare’s invented characters include [[Roderigo]], a young, disappointed suitor of [[Desdemona]], and Brabanzio, [[Desdemona]]’s father, who opposes her [[Marriage\|marriage]] to [[Othello]]. [[Bianca]], [[Cassio]]’s mistress, is developed from a few hints in the source. Shakespeare also introduces the military action between Turkey and [[Venice]]—infidels and Christians—which gives especial importance to [[Othello]]’s posting to Cyprus, a Venetian protectorate which the Turks attacked in 1570 and conquered in the following year. In the source, [[Othello]] and [[Desdemona]] are already happily settled into married life when they go to Cyprus; Shakespeare compresses the [[Time\|time]]-scheme and makes many changes to the narrative.<br>[[Othello]], a great [[success]] in Shakespeare’s [[Time\|time]], was one of the first plays to be acted after the reopening of the theatres in 1660, and since that [[Time\|time]] has remained one of the most popular plays on the English stage. |
| THE PERSONS OF THE | PLAY<br>[[Othello\|OTHELLO]], the Moor of [[Venice]]<br>[[Desdemona\|DESDEMONA]], his wife<br>[[Michael]] [[Cassio\|CASSIO]], his lieutenant<br>[[Bianca\|BIANCA]], a courtesan, in [[Love\|love]] with [[Cassio]]<br>[[Iago\|IAGO]], the Moor’s ensign<br>[[Emilia\|EMILIA]], [[Iago]]’s wife<br>A CLOWN, a servant of [[Othello]]<br>The DUKE of [[Venice]]<br>BRABANZIO, [[Desdemona]]’s father, a Senator of [[Venice]]<br>GRAZIANO, Brabanzio’s brother<br>LODOVICO, kinsman of Brabanzio |
| SENATORS | of [[Venice]]<br>[[Roderigo\|RODERIGO]], a Venetian gentleman, in [[Love\|love]] with [[Desdemona]]<br>MONTANO, Governor of Cyprus<br>A HERALD<br>A MESSENGER<br>Attendants, officers, sailors, gentlemen of Cyprus, musicians |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_1--scene_1" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act I, Scene 1" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="scene"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Duke, [[Escalus]], and other lords DUKE [[Escalus]]. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | My lord. |
| DUKE | Of [[government]] the properties to unfold<br>Would seem in me t’affect speech and discourse,<br>Since I am put to know that your own science<br>Exceeds in that the lists of all advice<br>My strength can give you. Then no more remains<br>But this: to your sufficiency, as your worth is able,<br>And let them work. The [[Nature\|nature]] of our people,<br>Our [[City\|city]]’s institutions and the terms<br>For common justice, you’re as pregnant in<br>As art and practice hath enriched any<br>That we remember.<br>He gives [[Escalus]] papers<br>There is our commission,<br>From which we would not have you warp.<br>(To a lord) Call hither,<br>I say bid come before us, [[Angelo]]. Exit lord<br><br>(To [[Escalus]]) What figure of us think you he will<br>bear?—<br>For you must know we have with special [[Soul\|soul]]<br>Elected him our absence to supply,<br>Lent him our terror, dressed him with our [[Love\|love]],<br>And given his deputation all the organs<br>Of our own power. What think you of it? |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | If any in Vienna be of worth<br>To undergo such ample grace and [[Honour\|honour]],<br>It is Lord [[Angelo]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Angelo]] |
| DUKE | Look where he comes. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Always obedient to your grace’s will,<br>I come to know your pleasure. |
| DUKE | [[Angelo]],<br>There is a kind of character in thy life<br>That to th‘observer doth thy [[history]]<br>Fully unfold. Thyself and thy belongings<br>Are not thine own so proper as to waste<br>Thyself upon thy virtues, they on thee.<br>Heaven doth with us as we with torches do,<br>Not light them for themselves; for if our virtues<br>Did not go forth of us, ’twere all alike<br>As if we had them not. Spirits are not finely touched<br>But to fine issues; nor [[Nature\|nature]] never lends<br>The smallest scruple of her excellence<br>But, like a thrifty goddess, she determines<br>Herself the glory of a creditor,<br>Both thanks and use. But 1 do bend my speech<br>To one that can my part in him advertise.<br>Hold therefore, [[Angelo]].<br>In our remove be thou at full ourself.<br>[[Mortality]] and [[Mercy\|mercy]] in Vienna<br>Live in thy tongue and heart. Old [[Escalus]],<br>Though first in question, is thy secondary.<br>Take thy commission. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Now good my lord,<br>Let there be some more test made of my metal<br>Before so noble and so great a figure<br>Be stamped upon it. |
| DUKE | No more evasion.<br>We have with leavened and prepared choice<br>Proceeded to you; therefore take your honours. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Angelo]] takes his commission |
| DUKE | Our haste from hence is of so quick condition<br>That it prefers itself, and [[Leaves\|leaves]] unquestioned<br>Matters of needful value. We shall write to you<br>As [[Time\|time]] and our concernings shall importune,<br>How it goes with us; and do look to know<br>What doth befall you here. So fare you [[Well\|well]].<br>To th’ hopeful execution do I leave you<br>Of your commissions. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Yet give leave, my lord,<br>That we may bring you something on the way. |
| DUKE | My haste may not admit it;<br>Nor need you, on mine [[Honour\|honour]], have to do<br>With any scruple. Your scope is as mine own,<br>So to enforce or qualify the laws<br>As to your [[Soul\|soul]] seems good. Give me your hand.<br>I’ll privily away. I [[Love\|love]] the people,<br>But do not like to stage me to their eyes.<br>Though it do [[Well\|well]], I do not relish [[Well\|well]]<br>Their loud applause and aves vehement;<br>Nor do I think the man of safe discretion<br>That does affect it. Once more, fare you [[Well\|well]]. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | The heavens give safety to your purposes! |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | Lead forth and bring you back in happiness! |
| DUKE | I thank you. Fare you [[Well\|well]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | I shall [[Desire\|desire]] you, sir, to give me leave<br>To have free speech with you; and it concerns me<br>To look into the [[Bottom\|bottom]] of my place.<br>A power I have, but of what strength and [[Nature\|nature]]<br>I am not yet instructed. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | ’Tis so with me. Let us withdraw together,<br>And we may soon our satisfaction have<br>Touching that point. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | I’ll wait upon your [[Honour\|honour]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_1--scene_2--01_exchange_dialogue" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act I, Scene 2 — exchange (dialogue) [1]" data-chapter="Scene 2" data-kind="exchange"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Lucio]], and two other Gentlemen |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | If the Duke with the other dukes come not to composition with the [[King]] of Hungary, why then, all the dukes fall upon the [[King]]. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Heaven grant us its peace, but not the [[King]] of Hungary’s! |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Amen. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Thou concludest like the sanctimonious pirate, that went to sea with the Ten Commandments, but scraped one out of the table. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | ‘Thou shalt not steal’? |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Ay, that he razed. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Why, ’twas a commandment to command the captain and all the rest from their functions: they put forth to steal. There’s not a soldier of us all that in the thanksgiving before meat do relish the petition [[Well\|well]] that prays for peace. 16 |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | I never heard any soldier dislike it. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | I believe thee, for I think thou never wast where grace was said. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | No? A dozen times at least. 20 |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | What, in metre? |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | In any proportion, or in any language. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | I think, or in any religion. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Ay, why not? Grace is grace despite of all controversy; as for example, thou thyself art a wicked [[Villain\|villain]] despite of all grace. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | [[Well]], there went but a pair of shears between us. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | I grant—as there may between the lists and the velvet. Thou art the list. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | And thou the velvet. Thou art good velvet, thou’rt a three-piled piece, I warrant thee. I had as lief be a list of an English kersey as be piled as thou art pilled, for a French velvet. Do I speak feelingly now? |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | I think thou dost, and indeed with most painful feeling of thy speech. I will out of thine own confession learn to begin thy health, but whilst I live forget to drink after thee. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | I think I have done myself wrong, have I not? 40 |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Yes, that thou hast, whether thou art tainted or free. Enter Mistress Overdone |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Behold, behold, where Madam Mitigation comes! I have purchased as many diseases under her roof as come to—45 |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | To what, I pray? |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Judge. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | To three thousand dolours a year? |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Ay, and more. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | A French [[Crown\|crown]] more. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Thou art always figuring diseases in me, but thou art full of error—I am sound. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Nay not, as one would say, healthy, but so sound as things that are hollow—thy bones are hollow, impiety has made a feast of thee. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | (to Mistress Overdone) How now, which of your hips has the most profound sciatica? |
| MISTRESS OVERDONE | [[Well]], [[Well\|well]]! There’s one yonder arrested and carried to [[Prison\|prison]] was worth five thousand of you all. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Who’s that, I pray thee? |
| MISTRESS OVERDONE | Marry sir, that’s [[Claudio]], Signor [[Claudio]]. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | [[Claudio]] to [[Prison\|prison]]? ’Tis not so. |
| MISTRESS OVERDONE | Nay, but I know ’tis so. I saw him arrested, saw him carried away; and, which is more, within these three days his head to be chopped off. |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_1--scene_2--02_exchange_lucio" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act I, Scene 2 — exchange (LUCIO) [2]" data-chapter="Scene 2" data-kind="exchange"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | But after all this fooling, I would not have it so. Art thou sure of this? |
| MISTRESS OVERDONE | I am too sure of it, and it is for getting Madame Julietta with [[Child\|child]]. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Believe me, this may be. He promised to meet me two hours since and he was ever precise in promise-keeping. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Besides, you know, it draws something near to the speech we had to such a purpose. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | But most of all agreeing with the proclamation. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Away; let’s go learn the [[Truth\|truth]] of it. Exeunt [[Lucio]] and Gentlemen |
| MISTRESS OVERDONE | Thus, what with the war, what with the sweat, what with the gallows, and what with [[Poverty\|poverty]], I am custom-shrunk. Enter [[Pompey]] How now, what’s the news with you? |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | You have not heard of the proclamation, have you? |
| MISTRESS OVERDONE | What proclamation, man? |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | All houses in the suburbs of Vienna must be plucked down. |
| MISTRESS OVERDONE | And what shall become of those in the [[City\|city]]? |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | They shall stand for seed. They had gone down too, but that a wise burgher put in for them. |
| MISTRESS OVERDONE | But shall all our houses of resort in the suburbs be pulled down? |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | To the ground, mistress. |
| MISTRESS OVERDONE | Why, here’s a change indeed in the commonwealth. What shall become of me? |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Come, [[Fear\|fear]] not you. Good counsellors lack no clients. Though you change your place, you need not change your trade. I’ll be your tapster still. [[Courage]], there will be pity taken on you. You that have worn your eyes almost out in the service, you will be considered. A noise within |
| MISTRESS OVERDONE | What’s to do here, Thomas Tapster? Let’s withdraw! Enter the Provost, [[Claudio]], [[Juliet]], and officers; [[Lucio]] and the two Gentlemen |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Here comes Signor [[Claudio]], led by the Provost to [[Prison\|prison]]; and there’s Madame [[Juliet]]. Exeunt Mistress Overdone and [[Pompey]] |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | (to the Provost) Fellow, why dost thou show me thus to th’ world? Bear me to [[Prison\|prison]], where I am committed. |
| PROVOST | I do it not in evil disposition, But from Lord [[Angelo]] by special charge. |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | Thus can the demigod Authority Make us pay down for our offence, by weight, The bonds of heaven. On whom it will, it will; On whom it will not, so; yet still ’tis just. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Why, how now, [[Claudio]]? Whence comes this restraint? |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | From too much liberty, my [[Lucio]], liberty. As surfeit is the father of much fast, So every scope, by the immoderate use, Turns to restraint. Our natures do pursue, Like rats that raven down their proper bane, A thirsty evil; and when we drink, we die. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | If I could speak so wisely under an arrest, I would send for certain of my creditors. And yet, to say the [[Truth\|truth]], I had as lief have the foppery of [[Freedom\|freedom]] as the [[morality]] of [[Imprisonment\|imprisonment]]. What’s thy offence, [[Claudio]]? |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | What but to speak of would offend again. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | What, is’t murder? |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | No. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Lechery? |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | Call it so. |
| PROVOST | Away, sir; you must go. |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_1--scene_2--03_exchange_claudio" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act I, Scene 2 — exchange (CLAUDIO) [3]" data-chapter="Scene 2" data-kind="exchange"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | One word, good friend. The Provost shows assent [[Lucio]], a word with you. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | A hundred, if they’ll do you any good. [[Claudio]] and [[Lucio]] speak apart Is lechery so looked after? |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | Thus stands it with me. Upon a true contract, I got possession of Julietta’s bed. You know the lady; she is fast my wife, Save that we do the denunciation lack Of outward order. This we came not to Only for propagation of a dower Remaining in the coffer of her friends, From whom we thought it meet to hide our [[Love\|love]] Till [[Time\|time]] had made them for us. But it chances The stealth of our most mutual entertainment With character too gross is writ on [[Juliet]]. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | With [[Child\|child]], perhaps? |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | Unhapp’ly even so. And the new deputy now for the Duke— Whether it be the fault and glimpse of newness, Or whether that the [[Body\|body]] public be A [[Horse\|horse]] whereon the governor doth ride, Who, newly in the seat, that it may know He can command, lets it straight feel the spur— Whether the tyranny be in his place, Or in his eminence that fills it up— I stagger in. But this new governor Awakes me all the enrolled penalties Which have, like unscoured armour, hung by th’ wall So long that fourteen zodiacs have gone round, And none of them been worn; and, for a name, Now puts the drowsy and neglected act Freshly on me. ’Tis surely for a name. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | I warrant it is; and thy head stands so tickle on thy shoulders that a milkmaid, if she be in [[Love\|love]], may sigh it off. Send after the Duke, and appeal to him. |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | I have done so, but he’s not to be found. I prithee, [[Lucio]], do me this kind service. This day my sister should the cloister enter, And there receive her approbation. Acquaint her with the danger of my state. Implore her in my voice that she make friends To the strict deputy. Bid herself assay him. I have great [[Hope\|hope]] in that, for in her youth There is a prone and speechless dialect Such as move men; beside, she hath prosperous art When she will play with reason and discourse, And [[Well\|well]] she can persuade. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | I pray she may—as [[Well\|well]] for the encouragement of thy like, which else would stand under grievous imposition, as for the enjoying of thy life, who I would be sorry should be thus foolishly lost at a game of tick-tack. I’ll to her. |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | I thank you, good friend [[Lucio]]. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Within two hours. |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | Come, officer; away. Exeunt [[Lucio]] and gentlemen at one door; [[Claudio]], [[Juliet]], Provost, and officers at another |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_1--scene_3" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act I, Scene 3" data-chapter="Scene 3" data-kind="scene"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Duke and a Friar |
| DUKE | No, holy father, throw away that thought.<br>Believe not that the dribbling dart of [[Love\|love]]<br>Can pierce a complete bosom. Why I [[Desire\|desire]] thee<br>To give me secret harbour hath a purpose<br>More [[Grave\|grave]] and wrinkled than the aims and ends<br>Of burning youth. |
| FRIAR | May your grace speak of it? |
| DUKE | My holy sir, none better knows than you<br>How I have ever loved the life removed,<br>And held in idle price to haunt assemblies<br>Where youth and cost a witless bravery keeps.<br>I have delivered to Lord [[Angelo]]—<br>A man of stricture and firm abstinence—<br>My absolute power and place here in Vienna;<br>And he supposes me travelled to Poland—<br>For so I have strewed it in the common ear,<br>And so it is received. Now, pious sir,<br>You will demand of me why I do this. |
| FRIAR | Gladly, my lord. |
| DUKE | We have strict statutes and most biting laws,<br>The needful bits and curbs to headstrong weeds,<br>Which for this fourteen years we have let slip;<br>Even like an o‘ergrown [[Lion\|lion]] in a [[Cave\|cave]]<br>That goes not out to prey. Now, as fond fathers,<br>Having bound up the threat’ning twigs of birch<br>Only to stick it in their children’s sight<br>For terror, not to use, in [[Time\|time]] the rod<br>More mocked becomes than feared: so our decrees,<br>Dead to infliction, to themselves are dead;<br>And Liberty plucks Justice by the nose,<br>The baby beats the [[Nurse\|nurse]], and quite athwart<br>Goes all decorum. |
| FRIAR | It rested in your grace<br>To unloose this tied-up Justice when you pleased,<br>And it in you more dreadful would have seemed<br>Than in Lord [[Angelo]]. |
| DUKE | I do [[Fear\|fear]], too dreadful.<br>Sith ‘twas my fault to give the people scope,<br>’Twould be my tyranny to strike and gall them<br>For what I bid them do—for we bid this be done<br>When evil deeds have their permissive pass,<br>And not the punishment. Therefore indeed, my father,<br>I have on [[Angelo]] imposed the office,<br>Who may in th‘ambush of my name strike [[Home\|home]],<br>And yet my [[Nature\|nature]] never in the fight<br>T’allow in slander. And to behold his sway,<br>I will as ’twere a brother of your order<br>Visit both prince and people. Therefore, I prithee,<br>Supply me with the habit, and instruct me<br>How I may formally in person bear<br>Like a true friar. More reasons for this action<br>At our more leisure shall I render you.<br>Only this one: Lord [[Angelo]] is precise,<br>Stands at a guard with envy, scarce confesses<br>That his [[Blood\|blood]] flows, or that his appetite<br>Is more to bread than stone. Hence shall we see<br>If power change purpose, what our seemers be. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_1--scene_4" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act I, Scene 4" data-chapter="Scene 4" data-kind="scene"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Isabella]], and Francesca, a nun |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | And have you nuns no farther privileges? |
| FRANCESCA | Are not these large enough? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Yes, truly. I speak not as desiring more,<br>But rather wishing a more strict restraint<br>Upon the sisterhood, the votarists of Saint Clare. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | (within)<br>Ho, peace be in this place! |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] to Francesca |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Who’s that which calls? |
| FRANCESCA | It is a man’s voice. Gentle [[Isabella]].<br>Turn you the key, and know his business of him.<br>You may, I may not; you are yet unsworn.<br>When you have vowed, you must not speak with men<br>But in the presence of the prioress.<br>Then if you speak, you must not show your face;<br>Or if you show your face, you must not speak.<br>[[Lucio]] calls within<br>He calls again. I pray you answer him. |
| *(didascalia)* | She stands asidel |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Peace and prosperity! Who is’t that calls?<br>She opens the door. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Lucio]] |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Hail, virgin, if you be—as those cheek-roses<br>Proclaim you are no less. Can you so stead me<br>As bring me to the sight of [[Isabella]],<br>A novice of this place, and the fair sister<br>To her unhappy brother [[Claudio]]? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Why her unhappy brother? Let me ask,<br>The rather for I now must make you know<br>I am that [[Isabella]], and his sister. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Gentle and fair, your brother kindly greets you.<br>Not to be weary with you, he’s in [[Prison\|prison]]. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Woe me! For what? |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | For that which, if myself might be his judge,<br>He should receive his punishment in thanks.<br>He hath got his friend with [[Child\|child]]. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Sir,<br>make me not your story. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | ‘Tis true. I would not—though ’tis my familiar sin<br>With maids to seem the lapwing, and to jest<br>Tongue far from heart—play with all virgins so.<br>I hold you as a thing enskied and sainted<br>By your renouncement, an immortal spirit,<br>And to be talked with in sincerity<br>As with a saint. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | You do blaspheme the good in mocking me. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Do not believe it. Fewness and [[Truth\|truth]], ’tis thus:<br>Your brother and his [[Lover\|lover]] have embraced.<br>As those that feed grow full, as blossoming [[Time\|time]]<br>That from the seedness the bare fallow brings<br>To teeming foison, even so her plenteous womb<br>Expresseth his full tilth and husbandry. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Someone with [[Child\|child]] by him? My cousin [[Juliet]]? |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Is she your cousin? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Adoptedly, as schoolmaids change their names<br>By vain though apt affection. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | She it is. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | O, let him marry her! |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | This is the point.<br>The Duke is very strangely gone from hence;<br>Bore many gentlemen—mysetf being one—<br>In hand and [[Hope\|hope]] of action; but we do learn,<br>By those that know the very nerves of state,<br>His giving out were of an infinite distance<br>From his true-meant design. Upon his place,<br>And with full line of his authority,<br>Governs Lord [[Angelo]]—a man whose [[Blood\|blood]]<br>Is very [[Snow\|snow]]-broth; one who never feels<br>The wanton stings and motions of the sense,<br>But doth rebate and blunt his natural edge<br>With profits of the mind, study, and fast.<br>He, to give [[Fear\|fear]] to use and liberty,<br>Which have for long run by the hideous law<br>As mice by lions, hath picked out an act<br>Under whose heavy sense your brother’s life<br>Falls into forfeit. He arrests him on it,<br>And follows close the rigour of the statute<br>To make him an example. All [[Hope\|hope]] is gone,<br>Unless you have the grace by your fair [[prayer]]<br>To soften [[Angelo]]. And that’s my pith<br>Of business ’twixt you and your poor brother. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Doth he so seek his life? |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Has censured him already,<br>And, as I hear, the Provost hath a warrant<br>For’s execution. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Alas, what poor<br>Ability’s in me to do him good? |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Assay the power you have. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | My power? Alas, I [[Doubt\|doubt]]. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | Our doubts are traitors,<br>And makes us lose the good we oft might win,<br>By fearing to attempt. Go to Lord [[Angelo]];<br>And let him learn to know, when maidens sue,<br>Men give like gods, but when they weep and kneel,<br>All their petitions are as freely theirs<br>As they themselves would owe them. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | I’ll see what I can do. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | But speedily. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | I will about it straight,<br>No longer staying but to give the Mother<br>Notice of my affair. I humbly thank you.<br>Commend me to my brother. Soon at [[Night\|night]]<br>I’ll send him certain word of my [[success]]. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | I take my leave of you. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Good sir, adieu. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Isabella]] and Francesca at one door, |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Lucio]] at another door |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_2--scene_1--01_exchange_dialogue" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act II, Scene 1 — exchange (dialogue) [1]" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="exchange"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Angelo]], [[Escalus]], and servants; a Justice |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | We must not make a scarecrow of the law, Setting it up to [[Fear\|fear]] the [[Birds\|birds]] of prey, And let it keep one shape till custom make it Their perch, and not their terror. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | Ay, but yet Let us be keen, and rather cut a little Than fall and bruise to [[Death\|death]]. Alas, this gentleman Whom I would save had a most noble father. Let but your [[Honour\|honour]] know— Whom I believe to be most strait in virtue— That in the working of your own affections, Had [[Time\|time]] cohered with place, or place with wishing, Or that the resolute acting of your [[Blood\|blood]] Could have attained th’effect of your own purpose— Whether you had not sometime in your life Erred in this point which now you censure him, And pulled the law upon you. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | ’Tis one thing to be tempted, [[Escalus]], Another thing to fall. I not deny The jury passing on the prisoner’s life May in the sworn twelve have a thief or two Guiltier than him they try. What knows the law That thieves do pass on thieves? What’s open made to justice, That justice seizes. ’Tis very pregnant: The [[Jewel\|jewel]] that we find, we stoop and take’t Because we see it, but what we do not see We tread upon and never think of it. You may not so extenuate his offence For I have had such faults; but rather tell me, When I that censure him do so offend, Let mine own judgement pattern out my [[Death\|death]], And nothing come in partial. Sir, he must die. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | Be it as your wisdom will. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Where is the Provost? Enter Provost |
| PROVOST | Here, if it like your [[Honour\|honour]]. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | See that [[Claudio]] Be execute by nine tomorrow morning. Bring him his confessor, let him be prepared, For that’s the utmost of his pilgrimage. Exit Provost |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | [[Well]], heaven forgive him, and forgive us all! Some rise by sin, and some by virtue fall. Some run from brakes of vice, and answer none; And some condemned for a fault [[Alone\|alone]]. Enter Elbow, Froth, [[Pompey]], and officers |
| ELBOW | Come, bring them away. If these be good people in a commonweal, that do nothing but use their abuses in common houses, I know no law. Bring them away. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | How now, sir? What’s your name? And what’s the matter? |
| ELBOW | If it please your [[Honour\|honour]], I am the poor Duke’s constable, and my name is Elbow. I do lean upon justice, sir; and do bring in here before your good [[Honour\|honour]] two notorious benefactors. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Benefactors? Welll What benefactors are they? Are they not malefactors? |
| ELBOW | If it please your [[Honour\|honour]], I know not [[Well\|well]] what they are; but precise villains they are, that I am sure of, and void of all profanation in the world that good Christians ought to have. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | (to [[Angelo]]) This comes off [[Well\|well]]; here’s a wise officer! |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Go to, what quality are they of? Elbow is your name? Why dost thou not speak, Elbow? |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | He cannot, sir; he’s out at elbow. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | What are you, sir? |
| ELBOW | He, sir? A tapster, sir, parcel bawd; one that serves a bad woman whose [[house]], sir, was, as they say, plucked down in the suburbs; and now she professes a hot-[[house]], which I think is a very ill [[house]] too. |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_2--scene_1--02_exchange_escalus" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act II, Scene 1 — exchange (ESCALUS) [2]" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="exchange"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | How know you that? |
| ELBOW | My wife, sir, whom I detest before heaven and your [[Honour\|honour]]— |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | How, thy wife? |
| ELBOW | Ay, sir, whom I thank heaven is an honest woman— |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | Dost thou detest her therefor? |
| ELBOW | I say, sir, I will detest myself also, as [[Well\|well]] as she, that this [[house]], if it be not a bawd’s [[house]], it is pity of her life, for it is a naughty [[house]]. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | How dost thou know that, constable? |
| ELBOW | Marry, sir, by my wife, who, if she had been a woman cardinally given, might have been accused in fornication, adultery, and all uncleanliness there. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | By the woman’s means? |
| ELBOW | Ay, sir, by Mistress Overdone’s means. But as she spit in his face, so she defied him. |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | (to [[Escalus]]) Sir, if it please your [[Honour\|honour]], this is not so. |
| ELBOW | Prove it before these varlets here, thou honourable man, prove it. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | (to [[Angelo]]) Do you hear how he misplaces? |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Sir, she came in great with [[Child\|child]], and [[Longing\|longing]]-saving your [[Honour\|honour]]’s reverence—for stewed prunes. Sir, we had but two in the [[house]], which at that very distant [[Time\|time]] stood, as it were, in a fruit dish—a dish of some threepence; your honours have seen such dishes; they are not china dishes, but very good dishes. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | Go to, go to, no matter for the dish, sir. |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | No, indeed, sir, not of a pin; you are therein in the right. But to the point. As I say, this Mistress Elbow, being, as I say, with [[Child\|child]], and being great-bellied, and [[Longing\|longing]], as I said, for prunes; and having but two in the dish, as I said, Master Froth here, this very man, having eaten the rest, as I said, and, as I say, paying for them very honestly; for, as you know, Master Froth, I could not give you threepence again. |
| FROTH | No, indeed. |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Very [[Well\|well]]. You being, then, if you be remembered, cracking the stones of the foresaid prunes— |
| FROTH | Ay, so I did indeed. |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Why, very [[Well\|well]].—I telling you then, if you be remembered, that such a one and such a one were past cure of the thing you wot of, unless they kept very good diet, as I told you— |
| FROTH | All this is true. no |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Why, very [[Well\|well]] then— |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | Come, you are a tedious [[Fool\|fool]]. To the purpose. What was done to Elbow’s wife that he hath cause to complain of? Come me to what was done to her. |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Sir, your [[Honour\|honour]] cannot come to that yet. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | No, sir, nor I mean it not. |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Sir, but you shall come to it, by your [[Honour\|honour]]’s leave. And I beseech you, look into Master Froth here, sir, a man of fourscore pound a year, whose father died at Hallowmas—was’t not at Hallowmas, Master Froth? |
| FROTH | All Hallow Eve. |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Why, very [[Well\|well]]. I [[Hope\|hope]] here be truths. He, sir, sitting, as I say, in a lower chair, sir—’twas in the Bunch of Grapes, where indeed you have a delight to sit, have you not? |
| FROTH | I have so, because it is an open room, and good for [[Winter\|winter]]. |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Why, very [[Well\|well]] then. I [[Hope\|hope]] here be truths. |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_2--scene_1--03_exchange_angelo" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act II, Scene 1 — exchange (ANGELO) [3]" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="exchange"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | This will last out a [[Night\|night]] in [[Russia]], When nights are longest there. (To [[Escalus]]) I’ll take my leave, And leave you to the hearing of the cause, Hoping you’ll find good cause to whip them all. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | I think no less. Good morrow to your lordship. Exit [[Angelo]] Now, sir, come on, what was done to Elbow’s wife, once more? |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Once, sir? There was nothing done to her once. |
| ELBOW | I beseech you, sir, ask him what this man did to my wife. |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | I beseech your [[Honour\|honour]], ask me. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | [[Well]], sir, what did this gentleman to her? |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | I beseech you, sir, look in this gentleman’s face. Good Master Froth, look upon his [[Honour\|honour]]. ’Tis for a good purpose. Doth your [[Honour\|honour]] mark his face? |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | Ay, sir, very [[Well\|well]]. |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Nay, I beseech you, mark it [[Well\|well]]. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | [[Well]], I do so. |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Doth your [[Honour\|honour]] see any harm in his face? |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | Why, no. |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | I’ll be supposed upon a book his face is the worst thing about him. Good, then—if his face be the worst thing about him, how could Master Froth do the constable’s wife any harm? I would know that of your [[Honour\|honour]]. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | He’s in the right, constable; what say you to it? |
| ELBOW | First, an it like you, the [[house]] is a respected [[house]]; next, this is a respected fellow; and his mistress is a respected woman. |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | (to [[Escalus]]) By this hand, sir, his wife is a more respected person than any of us all. |
| ELBOW | Varlet, thou liest; thou liest, wicked varlet. The [[Time\|time]] is yet to come that she was ever respected with man, woman, or [[Child\|child]]. |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Sir, she was respected with him before he married with her. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | Which is the wiser here, justice or iniquity? (To Elbow) Is this true? |
| ELBOW | (to [[Pompey]]) O thou caitiff, O thou varlet, O thou wicked Hanniball I respected with her before I was married to her? (To [[Escalus]]) If ever I was respected with her, or she with me, let not your worship think me the poor Duke’s officer. (To [[Pompey]]) Prove this, thou wicked Hannibal, or I’ll have mine action of battery on thee. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | If he took you a box o’th’ ear you might have your action of slander too. |
| ELBOW | Marry, I thank your good worship for it. What is’t your worship’s pleasure I shall do with this wicked caitiff? |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | Truly, officer, because he hath some offences in him that thou wouldst discover if thou couldst, let him continue in his courses till thou knowest what they are. |
| ELBOW | Marry, I thank your worship for it.—Thou seest, thou wicked varlet now, what’s come upon thee. Thou art to continue now, thou varlet, thou art to continue. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | (to Froth) Where were you born, friend? |
| FROTH | Here in Vienna, sir. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | Are you of fourscore pounds a year? |
| FROTH | Yes, an’t please you, sir. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | So. (To [[Pompey]]) What trade are you of, sir? |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | A tapster, a poor widow’s tapster. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | Your mistress’s name? |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Mistress Overdone. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | Hath she had any more than one husband? |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_2--scene_1--04_exchange_pompey" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act II, Scene 1 — exchange (POMPEY) [4]" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="exchange"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Nine, sir—Overdone by the last. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | Nine?—Come hither to me, Master Froth. Master Froth, I would not have you acquainted with tapsters. They will draw you, Master Froth, and you will hang them. Get you gone, and let me hear no more of you. |
| FROTH | I thank your worship. For mine own part, I never come into any room in a tap-[[house]] but I am drawn in. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | [[Well]], no more of it, Master Froth. Farewell. Exit Froth Come you hither to me, Master Tapster. What’s your name, Master Tapster? |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | [[Pompey]]. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | What else? |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Bum, sir. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | Troth, and your bum is the greatest thing about you; so that, in the beastliest sense, you are [[Pompey]] the Great. [[Pompey]], you are partly a bawd, [[Pompey]], howsoever you colour it in being a tapster, are you not? Come, tell me true; it shall be the better for you. |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Truly, sir, I am a poor fellow that would live. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | How would you live, [[Pompey]]? By being a bawd? What do you think of the trade, [[Pompey]]? Is it a lawful trade? |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | If the law would allow it, sir. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | But the law will not allow it, [[Pompey]]; nor it shall not be allowed in Vienna. |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Does your worship mean to geld and spay all the youth of the [[City\|city]]? |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | No, [[Pompey]]. |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | Truly, sir, in my poor opinion they will to’t then. If your worship will take order for the drabs and the knaves, you need not to [[Fear\|fear]] the bawds. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | There is pretty orders beginning, I can tell you. It is but heading and hanging. |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | If you head and hang all that offend that way but for ten year together, you’ll be glad to give out a commission for more heads. If this law hold in Vienna ten year, I’ll rent the fairest [[house]] in it after threepence a bay. If you live to see this come to pass, say [[Pompey]] told you so. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | Thank you, good [[Pompey]]; and in requital of your prophecy, hark you. I advise you, let me not find you before me again upon any complaint whatsoever; no, not for dwelling where you do. If I do, [[Pompey]], I shall beat you to your tent, and prove a shrewd Caesar to you; in plain dealing, [[Pompey]], I shall have you whipped. So for this [[Time\|time]], [[Pompey]], fare you [[Well\|well]]. |
| [[Pompey\|POMPEY]] | I thank your worship for your good counsel; aside but I shall follow it as the flesh and fortune shall better determine. Whip me? No, no; let carman whip his jade. The valiant heart’s not whipped out of his trade. Exit |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | Come hither to me, Master Elbow; come hither, Master Constable. How long have you been in this place of constable? |
| ELBOW | Seven year and a half, sir. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | I thought, by the readiness in the office, you had continued in it some [[Time\|time]]. You say seven years together? |
| ELBOW | And a half, sir. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | Alas, it hath been great pains to you. They do you wrong to put you so oft upon’t. Are there not men in your ward sufficient to serve it? |
| ELBOW | [[Faith]], sir, few of any wit in such matters. As they are chosen, they are glad to choose me for them. I do it for some piece of money, and go through with all. |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_2--scene_1--05_exchange_escalus" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act II, Scene 1 — exchange (ESCALUS) [5]" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="exchange"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | Look you bring me in the names of some six or seven, the most sufficient of your parish. |
| ELBOW | To your worship’s [[house]], sir? |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | To my [[house]]. Fare you [[Well\|well]]. Exit Elbow with officers What’s o’[[Clock\|clock]], think you? |
| JUSTICE | Eleven, sir. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | I pray you [[Home\|home]] to dinner with me. |
| JUSTICE | I humbly thank you. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | It grieves me for the [[Death\|death]] of [[Claudio]], But there’s no remedy. |
| JUSTICE | Lord [[Angelo]] is severe. |
| [[Escalus\|ESCALUS]] | It is but needful. [[Mercy]] is not itself that oft looks so. Pardon is still the [[Nurse\|nurse]] of second woe. But yet, poor [[Claudio]]! There is no remedy. Come, sir. Exeunt |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_2--scene_2--01_exchange_dialogue" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act II, Scene 2 — exchange (dialogue) [1]" data-chapter="Scene 2" data-kind="exchange"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Provost and a Servant |
| SERVANT | He’s hearing of a cause; he will come straight. I’ll tell him of you. |
| PROVOST | Pray you do. Exit Servant I’ll know His pleasure; maybe he will relent. Alas, He hath but as offended in [[A Dream\|a dream]]. All sects, all ages, smack of this vice; and he To die for’t! Enter [[Angelo]] |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Now, what’s the matter, Provost? |
| PROVOST | Is it your will [[Claudio]] shall die tomorrow? |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Did not I tell thee yea? Hadst thou not order? Why dost thou ask again? |
| PROVOST | Lest I might be too rash. Under your good correction, I have seen When after execution judgement hath Repented o’er his doom. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Go to; let that be mine. Do you your office, or give up your place, And you shall [[Well\|well]] be spared. |
| PROVOST | I crave your [[Honour\|honour]]’s pardon. What shall be done, sir, with the groaning [[Juliet]]? She’s very near her hour. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Dispose of her To some more fitter place, and that with [[Speed\|speed]]. Enter Servant |
| SERVANT | Here is the sister of the man condemned Desires access to you. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Hath he a sister? |
| PROVOST | Ay, my good lord; a very virtuous maid, And to be shortly of a sisterhood, If not already. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | [[Well]], let her be admitted. Exit Servant See you the fornicatress be removed. Let her have needful but not lavish means. There shall be order for’t. Enter [[Lucio]] and [[Isabella]] |
| PROVOST | God save your [[Honour\|honour]]. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Stay a little while. (To [[Isabella]]) You’re welcome. What’s your will? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | I am a woeful suitor to your [[Honour\|honour]]. Please but your [[Honour\|honour]] hear me. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | [[Well]], what’s your suit? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | There is a vice that most I do abhor, And most [[Desire\|desire]] should meet the blow of justice, For which I would not plead, but that I must; For which I must not plead, but that I am At war ’twixt will and will not. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | [[Well]], the matter? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | I have a brother is condemned to die. I do beseech you, let it be his fault, And not my brother. |
| PROVOST | (aside) Heaven give thee moving graces! |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Condemn the fault, and not the actor of it? Why, every fault’s condemned ere it be done. Mine were the very cipher of a function, To fine the faults whose fine stands in record, And let go by the actor. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | O just but severe law! I had a brother, then. Heaven keep your [[Honour\|honour]]. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | (aside to [[Isabella]]) Give’t not o’er so. To him again; entreat him. Kneel down before him; hang upon his gown. You are too cold. If you should need a pin, You could not with more tame a tongue [[Desire\|desire]] it. To him, I say! |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | (to [[Angelo]]) Must he needs die? |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Maiden, no remedy. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Yes, I do think that you might pardon him, And neither heaven nor man grieve at the [[Mercy\|mercy]]. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | I will not do’t. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | But can you if you would? |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Look what I will not, that I cannot do. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | But might you do’t, and do the world no wrong, If so your heart were touched with that remorse As mine is to him? |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | He’s sentenced; ’tis too late. |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_2--scene_2--02_exchange_lucio" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act II, Scene 2 — exchange (LUCIO) [2]" data-chapter="Scene 2" data-kind="exchange"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | (aside to [[Isabella]]) You are too cold. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Too late? Why, no; I that do speak a word May call it again. [[Well]], believe this, No ceremony that to great ones ’longs, Not the [[King\|king]]’s [[Crown\|crown]], nor the deputed [[Sword\|sword]], The marshal’s truncheon, nor the judge’s robe, Become them with one half so good a grace As [[Mercy\|mercy]] does. If he had been as you and you as he, You would have slipped like him, but he, like you, Would not have been so stern. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Pray you be gone. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | I would to heaven I had your potency, And you were Isabel! Should it then be thus? No; I would tell what ’twere to be a judge, And what a prisoner. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | (aside to [[Isabella]]) Ay, touch him; there’s the vein. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Your brother is a forfeit of the law, And you but waste your words. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Alas, alas! Why, all the souls that were were forfeit once, And He that might the vantage best have took Found out the remedy. How would you be If He which is the top of judgement should But judge you as you are? O, think on that, And [[Mercy\|mercy]] then will breathe within your lips, Like man new made. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Be you content, fair maid. It is the law, not I, condemn your brother. Were he my kinsman, brother, or my son, It should be thus with him. He must die tomorrow. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Tomorrow? O, that’s sudden! Spare him, spare him! He’s not prepared for [[Death\|death]]. Even for our kitchens We kill the fowl of season. Shall we serve heaven With less respect than we do minister To our gross selves? Good good my lord, bethink you: Who is it that hath died for this offence? There’s many have committed it. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | (aside) Ay, [[Well\|well]] said. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | The law hath not been dead, though it hath slept. Those many had not dared to do that evil If the first that did th‘edict infringe Had answered for his deed. Now ’tis awake, Takes note of what is done, and, like a [[Prophet\|prophet]], Looks in a glass that shows what future evils, Either raw, or by remissness new conceived And so in progress to be hatched and born, Are now to have no successive degrees, But ere they live, to end. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Yet show some pity. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | I show it most of all when I show justice, For then I pity those I do not know Which a dismissed offence would after gall, And do him right that, answering one foul wrong, Lives not to act another. Be satisfied. Your brother dies tomorrow. Be content. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | So you must be the first that gives this sentence, And he that suffers. O, it is excellent To have a giant’s strength, but it is tyrannous To use it like a giant. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | (aside to [[Isabella]]) That’s [[Well\|well]] said. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Could great men thunder As Jove himself does, Jove would never be quiet, For every pelting petty officer Would use his heaven for thunder, nothing but thunder. Merciful heaven, Thou rather with thy sharp and sulphurous bolt Split’st the unwedgeable and gnarlèd oak Than the soft myrtle. But man, proud man, Dressed in a little brief authority, Most ignorant of what he’s most assured, His glassy essence, like an angry ape Plays such fantastic tricks before high heaven As makes the angels weep, who, with our spleens, Would all themselves laugh mortal. |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_2--scene_2--03_exchange_lucio" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act II, Scene 2 — exchange (LUCIO) [3]" data-chapter="Scene 2" data-kind="exchange"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | (aside to [[Isabella]]) O, to him, to him, wench! He will relent. He’s coming; I perceive’t. |
| PROVOST | (aside) Pray heaven she win him! |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | We cannot weigh our brother with ourself. Great men may jest with saints; ’tis wit in them, But in the less, foul profanation. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | (aside to [[Isabella]]) Thou’rt i’th’ right, girl. More o’ that. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | That in the captain’s but a choleric word, Which in the soldier is flat blasphemy. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | (aside to [[Isabella]]) Art advised o’ that? More on’t. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Why do you put these sayings upon me? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Because authority, though it err like others, Hath yet a kind of medicine in itself That skins the vice o’th’ top. Go to your bosom; Knock there, and ask your heart what it doth know That’s like my brother’s fault. If it confess A natural guiltiness, such as is his, Let it not sound a thought upon your tongue Against my brother’s life. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | (aside) She speaks, and ’tis such sense That my sense breeds with it. (To [[Isabella]]) Fare you [[Well\|well]]. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Gentle my lord, turn back. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | I will bethink me. Come again tomorrow. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Hark how I’ll bribe you; good my lord, turn back. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | How, bribe me? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Ay, with such gifts that heaven shall share with you. |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | (aside to [[Isabella]]) You had marred all else. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Not with fond shekels of the tested gold, Or stones, whose rate are either rich or poor As fancy values them; but with true prayers, That shall be up at heaven and enter there Ere sunrise, prayers from preserved souls, From fasting maids whose minds are dedicate To nothing temporal. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | [[Well]], come to me tomorrow |
| [[Lucio\|LUCIO]] | (aside to [[Isabella]]) Go to; ’tis [[Well\|well]]; away. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Heaven keep your [[Honour\|honour]] safe. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | (aside) Amen; For I am that way going to [[Temptation\|temptation]], Where [[prayer]] is crossed. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | At what hour tomorrow Shall I attend your lordship? |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | At any [[Time\|time]] fore [[Noon\|noon]]. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | God save your [[Honour\|honour]]. |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_2--scene_2--04_monologue_angelo" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act II, Scene 2 — monologue (ANGELO) [4]" data-chapter="Scene 2" data-kind="monologue"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | (aside) From thee; even from thy virtue. Exeunt [[Isabella]], [[Lucio]], and Provost What’s this? What’s this? Is this her fault or mine? The tempter or the tempted, who sins most, ha? Not she; nor doth she tempt; but it is I That, lying by the violet in the sun, Do, as the carrion does, not as the flower, Corrupt with virtuous season. Can it be That modesty may more betray our sense Than woman’s lightness? Having waste ground enough, Shall we [[Desire\|desire]] to raze the sanctuary, And pitch our evils there? O, fie, fie, fie! What dost thou, or what art thou, [[Angelo]]? Dost thou [[Desire\|desire]] her foully for those things That make her good? O, let her brother live! Thieves for their robbery have authority, When judges steal themselves. What, do I [[Love\|love]] her, That I [[Desire\|desire]] to hear her speak again, And feast upon her eyes? What is’t I dream on? O cunning enemy, that, to catch a saint, With saints dost bait thy hook! Most dangerous Is that [[Temptation\|temptation]] that doth goad us on To sin in loving virtue. Never could the strumpet, With all her [[Double\|double]] vigour—art and [[Nature\|nature]]— Once stir my temper; but this virtuous maid Subdues me quite. Ever till now When men were fond, I smiled, and wondered how. Exit |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_2--scene_3--01_exchange_dialogue" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act II, Scene 3 — exchange (dialogue) [1]" data-chapter="Scene 3" data-kind="exchange"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter  at one door the Duke, disguised as a friar, and  at another door the Provost |
| DUKE | Hail to you, Provost!—so I think you are. |
| PROVOST | I am the Provost. What’s your will, good friar? |
| DUKE | Bound by my [[Charity\|charity]] and my blest order, I come to visit the afflicted spirits Here in the [[Prison\|prison]]. Do me the common right To let me see them, and to make me know The [[Nature\|nature]] of their crimes, that I may minister To them accordingly. |
| PROVOST | I would do more than that, if more were needful. Enter [[Juliet]] Look, here comes one, a gentlewoman of mine, Who, falling in the flaws of her own youth, Hath blistered her report. She is with [[Child\|child]], And he that got it, sentenced—a young man More fit to do another such offence Than die for this. |
| DUKE | When must he die? |
| PROVOST | As I do think, tomorrow. (To [[Juliet]]) I have provided for you. Stay a while, And you shall be conducted. |
| DUKE | Repent you, fair one, of the sin you carry? |
| [[Juliet\|JULIET]] | I do, and bear the shame most patiently. |
| DUKE | I’ll teach you how you shall arraign your [[Conscience\|conscience]], And try your penitence if it be sound Or hollowly put on. |
| [[Juliet\|JULIET]] | I’ll gladly learn. |
| DUKE | [[Love]] you the man that wronged you? |
| [[Juliet\|JULIET]] | Yes, as I [[Love\|love]] the woman that wronged him. |
| DUKE | So then it seems your most offenceful act Was mutually committed? |
| [[Juliet\|JULIET]] | Mutually. |
| DUKE | Then was your sin of heavier kind than his. |
| [[Juliet\|JULIET]] | I do confess it and repent it, father. |
| DUKE | ’Tis meet so, daughter. But lest you do repent As that the sin hath brought you to this shame— Which sorrow is always toward ourselves, not heaven, Showing we would not spare heaven as we [[Love\|love]] it, But as we stand in [[Fear\|fear]]— |
| [[Juliet\|JULIET]] | I do repent me as it is an evil, And take the shame with joy. |
| DUKE | There rest. Your partner, as I hear, must die tomorrow, And I am going with instruction to him. Grace go with you. Benedicite! Exit |
| [[Juliet\|JULIET]] | Must die tomorrow? O injurious law, That respites me a life whose very comfort Is still a dying horror! |
| PROVOST | ’Tis pity of him. Exeunt |
| PROVOST | 2.4 Enter [[Angelo]] |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | When I would pray and think, I think and pray To several subjects: heaven hath my empty words, Whilst my invention, hearing not my tongue, Anchors on Isabel; God in my mouth, As if I did but only chew his name, And in my heart the strong and swelling evil Of my conception. The state whereon I studied Is like a good thing, being often read, Grown seared and tedious. Yea, my gravity, Wherein—let no man hear me—I take [[Pride\|pride]], Could I with boot change for an idle plume Which the air beats in vain. O place, O form, How often dost thou with thy case, thy habit, Wrench awe from fools, and tie the wiser souls To thy false seeming! [[Blood]], thou art [[Blood\|blood]]. Let’s write ‘good angel’ on the devil’s horn— ’Tis now the devil’s crest. Enter Servant How now? Who’s there? |
| SERVANT | One Isabel, a sister, desires access to you. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Teach her the way. Exit Servant O heavens, Why does my [[Blood\|blood]] thus muster to my heart, Making both it unable for itself, And dispossessing all my other parts Of necessary fitness? So play the foolish throngs with one that swoons— Come all to help him, and so stop the air By which he should revive—and even so The general subject to a [[Well\|well]]-wished [[King\|king]] Quit their own part and, in obsequious fondness, Crowd to his presence, where their untaught [[Love\|love]] Must needs appear offence. Enter [[Isabella]] How now, fair maid? |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_2--scene_3--02_exchange_isabella_i" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act II, Scene 3 — exchange (ISABELLA I) [2]" data-chapter="Scene 3" data-kind="exchange"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | I am come to know your pleasure. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | (aside) That you might know it would much better please me Than to demand what ’tis. (To [[Isabella]]) Your brother cannot live. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Even so. Heaven keep your [[Honour\|honour]]. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Yet may he live a while, and it may be As long as you or I. Yet he must die. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Under your sentence? |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Yea. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | When, I beseech you?—that in his reprieve, Longer or shorter, he may be so fitted That his [[Soul\|soul]] sicken not. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Ha, fie, these filthy vices! It were as good To pardon him that hath from [[Nature\|nature]] stolen A man already made, as to remit Their saucy sweetness that do coin God’s image In stamps that are forbid. ’Tis all as easy Falsely to take away a life true made As to put metal in restrained moulds, To make a false one. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | ’Tis set down so in heaven, but not in earth. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Say you so? Then I shall pose you quickly. Which had you rather: that the most just law Now took your brother’s life, or, to redeem him, Give up your [[Body\|body]] to such sweet uncleanness As she that he hath stained? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Sir, believe this. I had rather give my [[Body\|body]] than my [[Soul\|soul]]. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | I talk not of your [[Soul\|soul]]. Our compelled sins Stand more for number than for account. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | How say you? |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Nay, I’ll not warrant that, for I can speak Against the thing I say. Answer to this. I now, the voice of the recorded law, Pronounce a sentence on your brother’s life. Might there not be a [[Charity\|charity]] in sin To save this brother’s life? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Please you to do’t, I’ll take it as a peril to my [[Soul\|soul]] It is no sin at all, but [[Charity\|charity]]. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Pleased you to do’t at peril of your [[Soul\|soul]] Were equal poise of sin and [[Charity\|charity]]. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | That I do beg his life, if it be sin, Heaven let me bear it. You granting of my suit, If that be sin, I’ll make it my morn [[prayer]] To have it added to the faults of mine, And nothing of your answer. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Nay, but hear me. Your sense pursues not mine. Either you are ignorant, Or seem so craftily, and that’s not good. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Let me be ignorant, and in nothing good But graciously to know I am no better. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Thus wisdom wishes to appear most bright When it doth tax itself: as these black masks Proclaim an enshield [[Beauty\|beauty]] ten times louder Than [[Beauty\|beauty]] could, displayed. But mark me. To be received plain, I’ll speak more gross. Your brother is to die. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | So. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | And his offence is so, as it appears, Accountant to the law upon that [[Pain\|pain]]. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | True. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Admit no other way to save his life— As I subscribe not that nor any other— But, in the loss of question, that you his sister, Finding yourself desired of such a person Whose credit with the judge, or own great place, Could fetch your brother from the manacles Of the all-binding law, and that there were No earthly mean to save him, but that either You must lay down the treasures of your [[Body\|body]] To this supposed, or else to let him suffer— What would you do? |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_2--scene_3--03_exchange_isabella" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act II, Scene 3 — exchange (ISABELLA) [3]" data-chapter="Scene 3" data-kind="exchange"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | As much for my poor brother as myself. That is, were I under the terms of [[Death\|death]], Th’impression of keen whips I’d wear as rubies, And strip myself to [[Death\|death]] as to a bed That [[Longing\|longing]] have been sick for, ere I’d yield My [[Body\|body]] up to shame. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Then must your brother die. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | And ’twere the cheaper way. Better it were a brother died at once Than that a sister, by redeeming him, Should die for ever. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Were not you then as cruel as the sentence That you have slandered so? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Ignominy in ransom and free pardon Are of two houses; lawful [[Mercy\|mercy]] Is nothing kin to foul redemption. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | You seemed of late to make the law a [[Tyrant\|tyrant]], And rather proved the sliding of your brother A merriment than a vice. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | O pardon me, my lord. It oft falls out To have what we would have, we speak not what we mean. I something do excuse the thing I hate For his advantage that I dearly [[Love\|love]]. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | We are all frail. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Else let my brother die— If not a federy, but only he, Owe and succeed thy weakness. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Nay, women are frail too. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Ay, as the glasses where they view themselves, Which are as easy broke as they make forms. Women? Help, heaven! Men their creation mar In profiting by them. Nay, call us ten times frail, For we are soft as our complexions are, And credulous to false prints. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | I think it [[Well\|well]], And from this testimony of your own sex, Since I suppose we are made to be no stronger Than faults may shake our frames, let me be bold. I do arrest your words. Be that you are; That is, a woman. If you be more, you’re none. If you be one, as you are [[Well\|well]] expressed By all external warrants, show it now, By putting on the destined livery. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | I have no tongue but one. Gentle my lord, Let me entreat you speak the former language. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Plainly conceive, I [[Love\|love]] you. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | My brother did [[Love\|love]] [[Juliet]], And you tell me that he shall die for it. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | He shall not, Isabel, if you give me [[Love\|love]]. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | I know your virtue hath a licence in’t, Which seems a little fouler than it is, To pluck on others. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Believe me, on mine [[Honour\|honour]], My words express my purpose. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Ha, little [[Honour\|honour]] to be much believed, And most pernicious purpose! Seeming, seeming! I will proclaim thee, [[Angelo]]; look for’t. Sign me a present pardon for my brother, Or with an outstretched throat I’ll tell the world aloud What man thou art. |
| [[Angelo\|ANGELO]] | Who will believe thee, Isabel? My unsoiled name, th‘austereness of my life, My vouch against you, and my place i’th’ state, Will so your accusation overweigh That you shall stifle in your own report, And smell of calumny. I have begun, And now I give my sensual race the rein. Fit thy consent to my sharp appetite. Lay by all nicety and prolixious blushes That banish what they sue for. Redeem thy brother By yielding up thy [[Body\|body]] to my will, Or else he must not only die the [[Death\|death]], But thy unkindness shall his [[Death\|death]] draw out To ling‘ring sufferance. Answer me tomorrow, Or by the affection that now guides me most, I’ll prove a [[Tyrant\|tyrant]] to him. As for you, Say what you can, my false o’erweighs your true. Exit |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_2--scene_3--04_exchange_isabella" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act II, Scene 3 — exchange (ISABELLA) [4]" data-chapter="Scene 3" data-kind="exchange"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | To whom should I complain? Did I tell this, Who would believe me? O perilous mouths, That bear in them one and the selfsame tongue Either of condemnation or approof, Bidding the law make curtsy to their will, Hooking both right and wrong to th’appetite, To follow as it draws! I’ll to my brother. Though he hath fall’n by prompture of the [[Blood\|blood]], Yet hath he in him such a mind of [[Honour\|honour]] That had he twenty heads to tender down On twenty bloody blocks, he’d yield them up Before his sister should her [[Body\|body]] stoop To such abhorred pollution. Then Isabel live chaste, and brother die: More than our brother is our chastity. I’ll tell him yet of [[Angelo]]’s request, And fit his mind to [[Death\|death]], for his [[Soul\|soul]]’s rest. Exit |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_3--scene_1--01_exchange_dialogue" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act III, Scene 1 — exchange (dialogue) [1]" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="exchange"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Duke, disguised as a friar, [[Claudio]], and the Provost |
| DUKE | So then you [[Hope\|hope]] of pardon from Lord [[Angelo]]? |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | The miserable have no other medicine But only [[Hope\|hope]]. I’ve [[Hope\|hope]] to live, and am prepared to die. |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_3--scene_1--02_monologue_duke" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act III, Scene 1 — monologue (DUKE) [2]" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="monologue"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| DUKE | Be absolute for [[Death\|death]]. Either [[Death\|death]] or life Shall thereby be the sweeter. Reason thus with life. If I do lose thee, I do lose a thing That none but fools would keep. A breath thou art, Servile to all the skyey influences That dost this habitation where thou keep‘st Hourly afflict. Merely thou art [[Death\|death]]’s [[Fool\|fool]], For him thou labour’st by thy flight to shun, And yet runn‘st toward him still. Thou art not noble, For all th’accommodations that thou bear’t Are nursed by baseness. Thou’rt by no means valiant, For thou dost [[Fear\|fear]] the soft and tender fork Of a poor worm. Thy best of rest is [[Sleep\|sleep]], And that thou oft provok‘st, yet grossly [[Fear\|fear]]’st Thy [[Death\|death]], which is no more. Thou art not thyself, For thou exist‘st on many a thousand grains That issue out of [[Dust\|dust]]. Happy thou art not, For what thou hast not, still thou striv’st to get, And what thou hast, forget‘st. Thou art not certain, For thy complexion shifts to strange effects After the [[Moon\|moon]]. If thou art rich, thou’rt poor, For like an ass whose back with ingots bows, Thou bear’st thy heavy riches but a [[Journey\|journey]], And [[Death\|death]] unloads thee. Friend hast thou none, For thine own bowels, which do call thee sire, The mere effusion of thy proper loins, Do curse the gout, serpigo, and the rheum, For ending thee no sooner. Thou hast nor youth nor age, But as it were an after-dinner’s [[Sleep\|sleep]] Dreaming on both; for all thy blessed youth Becomes as aged, and doth beg the alms Of palsied eld; and when thou art old and rich, Thou hast neither heat, affection, limb, nor [[Beauty\|beauty]], To make thy riches pleasant. What’s in this That bears the name of life? Yet in this life Lie hid more thousand deaths; yet [[Death\|death]] we [[Fear\|fear]] That makes these odds all even. |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_3--scene_1--03_exchange_claudio" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act III, Scene 1 — exchange (CLAUDIO) [3]" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="exchange"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | I humbly thank you. To sue to live, I find I seek to die, And seeking [[Death\|death]], find life. Let it come on. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | (within) What ho! Peace here, grace, and good company! |
| PROVOST | Who’s there? Come in; the wish deserves a welcome. |
| DUKE | (to [[Claudio]]) Dear sir, ere long I’ll visit you again. |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | Most holy sir, I thank you. Enter [[Isabella]] |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | My business is a word or two with [[Claudio]]. |
| PROVOST | And very welcome. Look, signor, here’s your sister. |
| DUKE | Provost, a word with you. |
| PROVOST | As many as you please. The Duke and Provost draw aside |
| DUKE | Bring me to hear them speak where I may be concealed. They conceal themselves |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | Now sister, what’s the comfort? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Why, as all comforts are: most good, most good indeed. Lord [[Angelo]], having affairs to heaven, Intends you for his swift ambassador, Where you shall be an everlasting leiger. Therefore your best appointment make with [[Speed\|speed]]. Tomorrow you set on. |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | Is there no remedy? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | None but such remedy as, to save a head, To cleave a heart in twain. |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | But is there any? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Yes, brother, you may live. There is a devilish [[Mercy\|mercy]] in the judge, If you’ll implore it, that will free your life, But fetter you till [[Death\|death]]. |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | Perpetual durance? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Ay, just, perpetual durance; a restraint, Though all the world’s vastidity you had, To a determined scope. |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | But in what [[Nature\|nature]]? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | In such a one as you consenting to’t Would bark your [[Honour\|honour]] from that trunk you bear, And leave you naked. |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | Let me know the point. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | O, I do [[Fear\|fear]] thee, [[Claudio]], and I quake Lest thou a feverous life shouldst entertain, And six or seven winters more respect Than a perpetual [[Honour\|honour]]. Dar’st thou die? The sense of [[Death\|death]] is most in apprehension, And the poor beetle that we tread upon In corporal sufferance finds a pang as great As when a giant dies. |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | Why give you me this shame? Think you I can a resolution fetch From flow’ry tenderness? If I must die, I will encounter darkness as a bride, And hug it in mine arms. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | There spake my brother; there my father’s [[Grave\|grave]] Did utter forth a voice. Yes, thou must die. Thou art too noble to conserve a life In base appliances. This outward-sainted deputy, Whose settled visage and deliberate word Nips youth i’th’ head and follies doth enew As falcon doth the fowl, is yet a devil. His filth within being cast, he would appear A pond as deep as hell. |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | The precise [[Angelo]]? |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | O, ‘tis the cunning livery of hell The damnedest [[Body\|body]] to invest and cover In precise guards! Dost thou think, [[Claudio]]: If I would yield him my virginity, Thou might’st be freed! |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | O heavens, it cannot be! |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Yes, he would give’t thee, from this rank offence, So to offend him still. This [[Night\|night]]’s the [[Time\|time]] That I should do what I abhor to name, Or else thou diest tomorrow. |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_3--scene_1--04_exchange_claudio" data-title="Measure for Measure — Act III, Scene 1 — exchange (CLAUDIO) [4]" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="exchange"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | Thou shalt not do’t. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | O, were it but my life, I’d throw it down for your deliverance As frankly as a pin. |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | Thanks, dear Isabel. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Be ready, [[Claudio]], for your [[Death\|death]] tomorrow. |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | Yes. Has he affections in him That thus can make him bite the law by th’ nose When he would force it? Sure it is no sin, Or of the deadly seven it is the least. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | Which is the least? |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | If it were damnable, he being so wise, Why would he for the momentary trick Be perdurably fined? O Isabel! |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | What says my brother? |
| [[Claudio\|CLAUDIO]] | [[Death]] is a fearful thing. |
| [[Isabella\|ISABELLA]] | And shamed life a hateful. |
